- **freedom-to-operate-analysis**: Assess whether building and selling a specific product or process risks infringing an active third-party patent — a different question from whether your own invention is novel. Use before committing to productize or commercialize anything patent-adjacent, or whenever the user asks "can we actually ship this without getting sued" or "is this clear to build." This is a technical infringement-risk scoping exercise, not legal advice — a formal opinion requires a patent attorney. - **freedom-to-operate-analyst**: Performs a structured freedom-to-operate triage for India under the Patents Act 1970, identifying in-force Indian patents from searches the user supplies, building element-by-element claim charts against the product, noting equivalents and validity attacks, and suggesting design-arounds, with compulsory licensing and working-statement angles. Use when a client asks whether a product can launch in India without infringing patents, or wants claim charts. Do not use where an infringement claim has already arisen; use ip-infringement-analyst instead.
